🏛️
KTU Exam Source: June 2023 University Exam (MAT102) • Original Paper Location: Part B • Question 20(b) (7 Marks)
👁️ View Question Paper
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$X(s) = \frac{s}{s^2 - 1} \implies x(t) = \cosh t; \quad Y(s) = \frac{1}{s^2 + 1} - \frac{1}{s^2 - 1} \implies y(t) = \sin t - \sinh t$$

Simultaneous Differential Systems: Coupled time-domain differential equations transform into a system of linear algebraic equations in $X(s)$ and $Y(s)$. Elimination reveals hyperbolic decoupling modes $\cosh t$ and $\sin t - \sinh t$.

⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$X(s) = \frac{s}{s^2 - 1} \implies x(t) = \cosh t; \quad Y(s) = \frac{1}{s^2 + 1} - \frac{1}{s^2 - 1} \implies y(t) = \sin t - \sinh t$$

Simultaneous Differential Systems: Coupled time-domain differential equations transform into a system of linear algebraic equations in $X(s)$ and $Y(s)$. Elimination reveals hyperbolic decoupling modes $\cosh t$ and $\sin t - \sinh t$.

⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$X(s) = \frac{s}{s^2 - 1} \implies x(t) = \cosh t; \quad Y(s) = \frac{1}{s^2 + 1} - \frac{1}{s^2 - 1} \implies y(t) = \sin t - \sinh t$$

Simultaneous Differential Systems: Coupled time-domain differential equations transform into a system of linear algebraic equations in $X(s)$ and $Y(s)$. Elimination reveals hyperbolic decoupling modes $\cosh t$ and $\sin t - \sinh t$.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

We are required to solve the system of simultaneous first-order ordinary differential equations: $$\frac{dx}{dt} + y = \sin t \quad \text{--- (1)}$$ $$\frac{dy}{dt} + x = \cos t \quad \text{--- (2)}$$ subject to the initial conditions: $$x(0) = 1, \quad y(0) = 0$$ using Laplace transforms.

**Step 1: Take Laplace Transforms of Both Equations** Let $\mathcal{L}\{x(t)\} = X(s)$ and $\mathcal{L}\{y(t)\} = Y(s)$. $$\mathcal{L}\left\{\frac{dx}{dt}\right\} = s X(s) - x(0) = s X(s) - 1$$ $$\mathcal{L}\left\{\frac{dy}{dt}\right\} = s Y(s) - y(0) = s Y(s) - 0 = s Y(s)$$

Right-hand sides: $$\mathcal{L}\{\sin t\} = \frac{1}{s^2 + 1}, \quad \mathcal{L}\{\cos t\} = \frac{s}{s^2 + 1}$$

Transformed system of algebraic equations: $$s X(s) - 1 + Y(s) = \frac{1}{s^2 + 1} \implies s X(s) + Y(s) = 1 + \frac{1}{s^2 + 1} = \frac{s^2 + 2}{s^2 + 1} \quad \text{--- (A)}$$ $$X(s) + s Y(s) = \frac{s}{s^2 + 1} \quad \text{--- (B)}$$

**Step 2: Eliminate $Y(s)$ to Solve for $X(s)$** Multiply Equation (A) by $s$: $$s^2 X(s) + s Y(s) = \frac{s(s^2 + 2)}{s^2 + 1} \quad \text{--- (C)}$$ Subtract Equation (B) from Equation (C): $$(s^2 - 1)X(s) = \frac{s(s^2 + 2)}{s^2 + 1} - \frac{s}{s^2 + 1} = \frac{s^3 + 2s - s}{s^2 + 1} = \frac{s^3 + s}{s^2 + 1} = \frac{s(s^2 + 1)}{s^2 + 1}$$ Cancel $(s^2 + 1)$ from numerator and denominator: $$(s^2 - 1)X(s) = s \implies X(s) = \frac{s}{s^2 - 1}$$

**Step 3: Solve for $Y(s)$** From Equation (B): $$s Y(s) = \frac{s}{s^2 + 1} - X(s) = \frac{s}{s^2 + 1} - \frac{s}{s^2 - 1} = s \left[ \frac{1}{s^2 + 1} - \frac{1}{s^2 - 1} \right]$$ Divide both sides by $s$: $$Y(s) = \frac{1}{s^2 + 1} - \frac{1}{s^2 - 1} = -\frac{1}{s^2 - 1} + \frac{1}{s^2 + 1}$$

**Step 4: Inverse Laplace Transforms** Recall the standard hyperbolic and trigonometric inverse transforms: $$\mathcal{L}^{-1}\left\{ \frac{s}{s^2 - 1} \right\} = \cosh t$$ $$\mathcal{L}^{-1}\left\{ \frac{1}{s^2 - 1} \right\} = \sinh t$$ $$\mathcal{L}^{-1}\left\{ \frac{1}{s^2 + 1} \right\} = \sin t$$

Inverting $X(s)$: $$x(t) = \mathcal{L}^{-1}\{X(s)\} = \cosh t$$

Inverting $Y(s)$: $$y(t) = \mathcal{L}^{-1}\{Y(s)\} = -\sinh t + \sin t = \sin t - \sinh t$$

**Step 5: Verification of Solutions** - Initial conditions: $$x(0) = \cosh(0) = 1 \quad (\text{Satisfied})$$ $$y(0) = \sin(0) - \sinh(0) = 0 - 0 = 0 \quad (\text{Satisfied})$$ - First differential equation check: $$\frac{dx}{dt} = \sinh t \implies \frac{dx}{dt} + y = \sinh t + (\sin t - \sinh t) = \sin t \quad (\text{Matches Eq 1})$$ - Second differential equation check: $$\frac{dy}{dt} = \cos t - \cosh t \implies \frac{dy}{dt} + x = (\cos t - \cosh t) + \cosh t = \cos t \quad (\text{Matches Eq 2})$$

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

We are required to solve the system of simultaneous first-order ordinary differential equations: $$\frac{dx}{dt} + y = \sin t \quad \text{--- (1)}$$ $$\frac{dy}{dt} + x = \cos t \quad \text{--- (2)}$$ subject to the initial conditions: $$x(0) = 1, \quad y(0) = 0$$ using Laplace transforms.

**Step 1: Take Laplace Transforms of Both Equations** Let $\mathcal{L}\{x(t)\} = X(s)$ and $\mathcal{L}\{y(t)\} = Y(s)$. $$\mathcal{L}\left\{\frac{dx}{dt}\right\} = s X(s) - x(0) = s X(s) - 1$$ $$\mathcal{L}\left\{\frac{dy}{dt}\right\} = s Y(s) - y(0) = s Y(s) - 0 = s Y(s)$$

Right-hand sides: $$\mathcal{L}\{\sin t\} = \frac{1}{s^2 + 1}, \quad \mathcal{L}\{\cos t\} = \frac{s}{s^2 + 1}$$

Transformed system of algebraic equations: $$s X(s) - 1 + Y(s) = \frac{1}{s^2 + 1} \implies s X(s) + Y(s) = 1 + \frac{1}{s^2 + 1} = \frac{s^2 + 2}{s^2 + 1} \quad \text{--- (A)}$$ $$X(s) + s Y(s) = \frac{s}{s^2 + 1} \quad \text{--- (B)}$$

**Step 2: Eliminate $Y(s)$ to Solve for $X(s)$** Multiply Equation (A) by $s$: $$s^2 X(s) + s Y(s) = \frac{s(s^2 + 2)}{s^2 + 1} \quad \text{--- (C)}$$ Subtract Equation (B) from Equation (C): $$(s^2 - 1)X(s) = \frac{s(s^2 + 2)}{s^2 + 1} - \frac{s}{s^2 + 1} = \frac{s^3 + 2s - s}{s^2 + 1} = \frac{s^3 + s}{s^2 + 1} = \frac{s(s^2 + 1)}{s^2 + 1}$$ Cancel $(s^2 + 1)$ from numerator and denominator: $$(s^2 - 1)X(s) = s \implies X(s) = \frac{s}{s^2 - 1}$$

**Step 3: Solve for $Y(s)$** From Equation (B): $$s Y(s) = \frac{s}{s^2 + 1} - X(s) = \frac{s}{s^2 + 1} - \frac{s}{s^2 - 1} = s \left[ \frac{1}{s^2 + 1} - \frac{1}{s^2 - 1} \right]$$ Divide both sides by $s$: $$Y(s) = \frac{1}{s^2 + 1} - \frac{1}{s^2 - 1} = -\frac{1}{s^2 - 1} + \frac{1}{s^2 + 1}$$

**Step 4: Inverse Laplace Transforms** Recall the standard hyperbolic and trigonometric inverse transforms: $$\mathcal{L}^{-1}\left\{ \frac{s}{s^2 - 1} \right\} = \cosh t$$ $$\mathcal{L}^{-1}\left\{ \frac{1}{s^2 - 1} \right\} = \sinh t$$ $$\mathcal{L}^{-1}\left\{ \frac{1}{s^2 + 1} \right\} = \sin t$$

Inverting $X(s)$: $$x(t) = \mathcal{L}^{-1}\{X(s)\} = \cosh t$$

Inverting $Y(s)$: $$y(t) = \mathcal{L}^{-1}\{Y(s)\} = -\sinh t + \sin t = \sin t - \sinh t$$

**Step 5: Verification of Solutions** - Initial conditions: $$x(0) = \cosh(0) = 1 \quad (\text{Satisfied})$$ $$y(0) = \sin(0) - \sinh(0) = 0 - 0 = 0 \quad (\text{Satisfied})$$ - First differential equation check: $$\frac{dx}{dt} = \sinh t \implies \frac{dx}{dt} + y = \sinh t + (\sin t - \sinh t) = \sin t \quad (\text{Matches Eq 1})$$ - Second differential equation check: $$\frac{dy}{dt} = \cos t - \cosh t \implies \frac{dy}{dt} + x = (\cos t - \cosh t) + \cosh t = \cos t \quad (\text{Matches Eq 2})$$

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പ്രാദേശിക പഠന സഹായി

We are required to solve the system of simultaneous first-order ordinary differential equations: $$\frac{dx}{dt} + y = \sin t \quad \text{--- (1)}$$ $$\frac{dy}{dt} + x = \cos t \quad \text{--- (2)}$$ subject to the initial conditions: $$x(0) = 1, \quad y(0) = 0$$ using Laplace transforms.

**Step 1: Take Laplace Transforms of Both Equations** Let $\mathcal{L}\{x(t)\} = X(s)$ and $\mathcal{L}\{y(t)\} = Y(s)$. $$\mathcal{L}\left\{\frac{dx}{dt}\right\} = s X(s) - x(0) = s X(s) - 1$$ $$\mathcal{L}\left\{\frac{dy}{dt}\right\} = s Y(s) - y(0) = s Y(s) - 0 = s Y(s)$$

Right-hand sides: $$\mathcal{L}\{\sin t\} = \frac{1}{s^2 + 1}, \quad \mathcal{L}\{\cos t\} = \frac{s}{s^2 + 1}$$

Transformed system of algebraic equations: $$s X(s) - 1 + Y(s) = \frac{1}{s^2 + 1} \implies s X(s) + Y(s) = 1 + \frac{1}{s^2 + 1} = \frac{s^2 + 2}{s^2 + 1} \quad \text{--- (A)}$$ $$X(s) + s Y(s) = \frac{s}{s^2 + 1} \quad \text{--- (B)}$$

**Step 2: Eliminate $Y(s)$ to Solve for $X(s)$** Multiply Equation (A) by $s$: $$s^2 X(s) + s Y(s) = \frac{s(s^2 + 2)}{s^2 + 1} \quad \text{--- (C)}$$ Subtract Equation (B) from Equation (C): $$(s^2 - 1)X(s) = \frac{s(s^2 + 2)}{s^2 + 1} - \frac{s}{s^2 + 1} = \frac{s^3 + 2s - s}{s^2 + 1} = \frac{s^3 + s}{s^2 + 1} = \frac{s(s^2 + 1)}{s^2 + 1}$$ Cancel $(s^2 + 1)$ from numerator and denominator: $$(s^2 - 1)X(s) = s \implies X(s) = \frac{s}{s^2 - 1}$$

**Step 3: Solve for $Y(s)$** From Equation (B): $$s Y(s) = \frac{s}{s^2 + 1} - X(s) = \frac{s}{s^2 + 1} - \frac{s}{s^2 - 1} = s \left[ \frac{1}{s^2 + 1} - \frac{1}{s^2 - 1} \right]$$ Divide both sides by $s$: $$Y(s) = \frac{1}{s^2 + 1} - \frac{1}{s^2 - 1} = -\frac{1}{s^2 - 1} + \frac{1}{s^2 + 1}$$

**Step 4: Inverse Laplace Transforms** Recall the standard hyperbolic and trigonometric inverse transforms: $$\mathcal{L}^{-1}\left\{ \frac{s}{s^2 - 1} \right\} = \cosh t$$ $$\mathcal{L}^{-1}\left\{ \frac{1}{s^2 - 1} \right\} = \sinh t$$ $$\mathcal{L}^{-1}\left\{ \frac{1}{s^2 + 1} \right\} = \sin t$$

Inverting $X(s)$: $$x(t) = \mathcal{L}^{-1}\{X(s)\} = \cosh t$$

Inverting $Y(s)$: $$y(t) = \mathcal{L}^{-1}\{Y(s)\} = -\sinh t + \sin t = \sin t - \sinh t$$

**Step 5: Verification of Solutions** - Initial conditions: $$x(0) = \cosh(0) = 1 \quad (\text{Satisfied})$$ $$y(0) = \sin(0) - \sinh(0) = 0 - 0 = 0 \quad (\text{Satisfied})$$ - First differential equation check: $$\frac{dx}{dt} = \sinh t \implies \frac{dx}{dt} + y = \sinh t + (\sin t - \sinh t) = \sin t \quad (\text{Matches Eq 1})$$ - Second differential equation check: $$\frac{dy}{dt} = \cos t - \cosh t \implies \frac{dy}{dt} + x = (\cos t - \cosh t) + \cosh t = \cos t \quad (\text{Matches Eq 2})$$
